    @pytest.fixture(scope="session")
    def any_location(client):
        """The id of a location that EXISTS in this register, whatever it is.
    
        Four location tests used to ask for `/api/v1/locations/1` outright. That
        passed for as long as the suite was pointed at one richly-seeded register
        and broke the moment handoff 25 moved it onto a marked test tenant, where
        row 1 is not a location. The id of a row is not a fact about the API — it
        is a fact about one database's insert order — so the tests now read one
        off the register instead of assuming it.
    
        Falls back to the fixture location, so this works on an empty tenant too.
        """
        r = client.get("/api/v1/locations", params={"limit": 1})
        if r.status_code == 200:
            items = r.json().get("data") or []
            if items:
                return items[0]["id"]
        created = client.post("/api/v1/locations",
                              json={"name": "pytest-any-location"})

tests/conftest.py:791: Failed
_ ERROR at setup of test_a_move_is_one_event_and_says_what_its_record_is_worth _

client = <tests.conftest.ApiClient object at 0x108d2bc50>

    @pytest.fixture(scope="session")
    def any_location(client):
        """The id of a location that EXISTS in this register, whatever it is.
    
        Four location tests used to ask for `/api/v1/locations/1` outright. That
        passed for as long as the suite was pointed at one richly-seeded register
        and broke the moment handoff 25 moved it onto a marked test tenant, where
        row 1 is not a location. The id of a row is not a fact about the API — it
        is a fact about one database's insert order — so the tests now read one
        off the register instead of assuming it.
    
        Falls back to the fixture location, so this works on an empty tenant too.
        """
        r = client.get("/api/v1/locations", params={"limit": 1})
        if r.status_code == 200:
            items = r.json().get("data") or []
            if items:
                return items[0]["id"]
        created = client.post("/api/v1/locations",
                              json={"name": "pytest-any-location"})
        if created.status_code != 201:
>           pytest.fail("no location exists and one could not be created: HTTP %s %s"
                        % (created.status_code, created.text[:200]))
E           Failed: no location exists and one could not be created: HTTP 401 {"error": {"code": "unauthorized", "message": "Invalid token", "request_id": "f9d2dedc8b8346e68d27e0ff32c948ee", "details": {"http_status": 401}}}

tests/conftest.py:791: Failed
_______________________ ERROR at setup of test_get_by_id _______________________

client = <tests.conftest.ApiClient object at 0x108d2bc50>

    @pytest.fixture(scope="session")
    def any_location(client):
        """The id of a location that EXISTS in this register, whatever it is.
    
        Four location tests used to ask for `/api/v1/locations/1` outright. That
        passed for as long as the suite was pointed at one richly-seeded register
        and broke the moment handoff 25 moved it onto a marked test tenant, where
        row 1 is not a location. The id of a row is not a fact about the API — it
        is a fact about one database's insert order — so the tests now read one
        off the register instead of assuming it.
    
        Falls back to the fixture location, so this works on an empty tenant too.
        """
        r = client.get("/api/v1/locations", params={"limit": 1})
        if r.status_code == 200:
            items = r.json().get("data") or []
            if items:
                return items[0]["id"]
        created = client.post("/api/v1/locations",
                              json={"name": "pytest-any-location"})
        if created.status_code != 201:
>           pytest.fail("no location exists and one could not be created: HTTP %s %s"
                        % (created.status_code, created.text[:200]))
E           Failed: no location exists and one could not be created: HTTP 401 {"error": {"code": "unauthorized", "message": "Invalid token", "request_id": "f9d2dedc8b8346e68d27e0ff32c948ee", "details": {"http_status": 401}}}

    def _make_request(
        self,
        conn: BaseHTTPConnection,
        method: str,
        url: str,
        body: _TYPE_BODY | None = None,
        headers: typing.Mapping[str, str] | None = None,
        retries: Retry | None = None,
        timeout: _TYPE_TIMEOUT = _DEFAULT_TIMEOUT,
        chunked: bool = False,
        response_conn: BaseHTTPConnection | None = None,
        preload_content: bool = True,
        decode_content: bool = True,
        enforce_content_length: bool = True,
    ) -> BaseHTTPResponse:
        """
        Perform a request on a given urllib connection object taken from our
        pool.
    
        :param conn:
            a connection from one of our connection pools
    
        :param method:
            HTTP request method (such as GET, POST, PUT, etc.)
    
        :param url:
            The URL to perform the request on.
    
        :param body:
            Data to send in the request body, either :class:`str`, :class:`bytes`,
            an iterable of :class:`str`/:class:`bytes`, or a file-like object.
    
        :param headers:
            Dictionary of custom headers to send, such as User-Agent,
            If-None-Match, etc. If None, pool headers are used. If provided,
            these headers completely replace any pool-specific headers.
    
        :param retries:
            Configure the number of retries to allow before raising a
            :class:`~urllib3.exceptions.MaxRetryError` exception.
    
            Pass ``None`` to retry until you receive a response. Pass a
            :class:`~urllib3.util.retry.Retry` object for fine-grained control
            over different types of retries.
            Pass an integer number to retry connection errors that many times,
            but no other types of errors. Pass zero to never retry.
    
            If ``False``, then retries are disabled and any exception is raised
            immediately. Also, instead of raising a MaxRetryError on redirects,
            the redirect response will be returned.
    
        :type retries: :class:`~urllib3.util.retry.Retry`, False, or an int.
    
        :param timeout:
            If specified, overrides the default timeout for this one
            request. It may be a float (in seconds) or an instance of
            :class:`urllib3.util.Timeout`.
    
        :param chunked:
            If True, urllib3 will send the body using chunked transfer
            encoding. Otherwise, urllib3 will send the body using the standard
            content-length form. Defaults to False.
    
        :param response_conn:
            Set this to ``None`` if you will handle releasing the connection or
            set the connection to have the response release it.
    
        :param preload_content:
          If True, the response's body will be preloaded during construction.
    
        :param decode_content:
            If True, will attempt to decode the body based on the
            'content-encoding' header.
    
        :param enforce_content_length:
            Enforce content length checking. Body returned by server must match
            value of Content-Length header, if present. Otherwise, raise error.
        """
        self.num_requests += 1
    
        timeout_obj = self._get_timeout(timeout)
        timeout_obj.start_connect()
        conn.timeout = Timeout.resolve_default_timeout(timeout_obj.connect_timeout)
    
        try:
            # Trigger any extra validation we need to do.
            try:
                self._validate_conn(conn)
            except (SocketTimeout, BaseSSLError) as e:
                self._raise_timeout(err=e, url=url, timeout_value=conn.timeout)
                raise
    
        # _validate_conn() starts the connection to an HTTPS proxy
        # so we need to wrap errors with 'ProxyError' here too.
        except (
            OSError,
            NewConnectionError,
            TimeoutError,
            BaseSSLError,
            CertificateError,
            SSLError,
        ) as e:
            new_e: Exception = e
            if isinstance(e, (BaseSSLError, CertificateError)):
                new_e = SSLError(e)
            # If the connection didn't successfully connect to it's proxy
            # then there
            if isinstance(
                new_e, (OSError, NewConnectionError, TimeoutError, SSLError)
            ) and (conn and conn.proxy and not conn.has_connected_to_proxy):
                new_e = _wrap_proxy_error(new_e, conn.proxy.scheme)
            raise new_e
    
        # conn.request() calls http.client.*.request, not the method in
        # urllib3.request. It also calls makefile (recv) on the socket.
        try:
            conn.request(
                method,
                url,
                body=body,
                headers=headers,
                chunked=chunked,
                preload_content=preload_content,
                decode_content=decode_content,
                enforce_content_length=enforce_content_length,
            )
    
        # We are swallowing BrokenPipeError (errno.EPIPE) since the server is
        # legitimately able to close the connection after sending a valid response.
        # With this behaviour, the received response is still readable.
        except BrokenPipeError:
            pass
        except OSError as e:
            # MacOS/Linux
            # EPROTOTYPE and ECONNRESET are needed on macOS
            # https://erickt.github.io/blog/2014/11/19/adventures-in-debugging-a-potential-osx-kernel-bug/
            # Condition changed later to emit ECONNRESET instead of only EPROTOTYPE.
            if e.errno != errno.EPROTOTYPE and e.errno != errno.ECONNRESET:
                raise
    
        # Reset the timeout for the recv() on the socket
        read_timeout = timeout_obj.read_timeout
    
        if not conn.is_closed:
            # In Python 3 socket.py will catch EAGAIN and return None when you
            # try and read into the file pointer created by http.client, which
            # instead raises a BadStatusLine exception. Instead of catching
            # the exception and assuming all BadStatusLine exceptions are read
            # timeouts, check for a zero timeout before making the request.
            if read_timeout == 0:
                raise ReadTimeoutError(
                    self, url, f"Read timed out. (read timeout={read_timeout})"
                )
            conn.timeout = read_timeout
    
        # Receive the response from the server
        try:
>           response = conn.getresponse()

    def send(
        self,
        request: PreparedRequest,
        stream: bool = False,
        timeout: _t.TimeoutType = None,
        verify: _t.VerifyType = True,
        cert: _t.CertType = None,
        proxies: dict[str, str] | None = None,
    ) -> Response:
        """Sends PreparedRequest object. Returns Response object.
    
        :param request: The :class:`PreparedRequest <PreparedRequest>` being sent.
        :param stream: (optional) Whether to stream the request content.
        :param timeout: (optional) How long to wait for the server to send
            data before giving up, as a float, or a :ref:`(connect timeout,
            read timeout) <timeouts>` tuple.
        :type timeout: float or tuple or urllib3 Timeout object
        :param verify: (optional) Either a boolean, in which case it controls whether
            we verify the server's TLS certificate, or a string, in which case it
            must be a path to a CA bundle to use
        :param cert: (optional) Any user-provided SSL certificate to be trusted.
        :param proxies: (optional) The proxies dictionary to apply to the request.
        :rtype: requests.Response
        """
    
        assert _is_prepared(request)
    
        try:
            conn = self.get_connection_with_tls_context(
                request, verify, proxies=proxies, cert=cert
            )
        except LocationValueError as e:
            raise InvalidURL(e, request=request)
    
        self.cert_verify(conn, request.url, verify, cert)
        url = self.request_url(request, proxies)
        self.add_headers(
            request,
            stream=stream,
            timeout=timeout,
            verify=verify,
            cert=cert,
            proxies=proxies,
        )
    
        chunked = not (request.body is None or "Content-Length" in request.headers)
    
        if isinstance(timeout, tuple):
            try:
                connect, read = timeout
                resolved_timeout = TimeoutSauce(connect=connect, read=read)
            except ValueError:
                raise ValueError(
                    f"Invalid timeout {timeout}. Pass a (connect, read) timeout tuple, "
                    f"or a single float to set both timeouts to the same value."
                )
        elif isinstance(timeout, TimeoutSauce):
            resolved_timeout = timeout
        else:
            resolved_timeout = TimeoutSauce(connect=timeout, read=timeout)
    
        try:
            resp = conn.urlopen(
                method=request.method,
                url=url,
                body=request.body,  # type: ignore[arg-type]  # urllib3 stubs don't accept Iterable[bytes | str]
                headers=request.headers,  # type: ignore[arg-type]  # urllib3#3072
                redirect=False,
                assert_same_host=False,
                preload_content=False,
                decode_content=False,
                retries=self.max_retries,
                timeout=resolved_timeout,
                chunked=chunked,
            )
    
        except (ProtocolError, OSError) as err:
            raise ConnectionError(err, request=request)
    
        except MaxRetryError as e:
            if isinstance(e.reason, ConnectTimeoutError):
                # TODO: Remove this in 3.0.0: see #2811
                if not isinstance(e.reason, NewConnectionError):
                    raise ConnectTimeout(e, request=request)
    
            if isinstance(e.reason, ResponseError):
                raise RetryError(e, request=request)
    
            if isinstance(e.reason, _ProxyError):
                raise ProxyError(e, request=request)
    
            if isinstance(e.reason, _SSLError):
                # This branch is for urllib3 v1.22 and later.
                raise SSLError(e, request=request)
    
            raise ConnectionError(e, request=request)
    
        except ClosedPoolError as e:
            raise ConnectionError(e, request=request)
    
        except _ProxyError as e:
            raise ProxyError(e)
    
        except (_SSLError, _HTTPError) as e:
            if isinstance(e, _SSLError):
                # This branch is for urllib3 versions earlier than v1.22
                raise SSLError(e, request=request)
            elif isinstance(e, ReadTimeoutError):
>               raise ReadTimeout(e, request=request)
E               requests.exceptions.ReadTimeout: HTTPConnectionPool(host='test19.localhost', port=8069): Read timed out. (read timeout=30)

    @pytest.mark.parametrize("state", ["active", "disposed"])
    def test_an_asset_that_is_not_missing_cannot_be_marked_found(
            client, test_location, state):
        """409 — there is nothing to find.
    
        For an ACTIVE asset a 200 would append no event, because the status
        does not change, and would tell the caller its sentence had been
        recorded. For a DISPOSED one it is the terminal rule's tenth door:
        `mark-found` writes `active`, which is a way out of `disposed` for
        anybody who thought to ask. The route answers before the model has to,
        and the record is compared WHOLE, as it is for every other closed door.
        """

tests/conftest.py:1034: AssertionError
____________ test_condemning_an_asset_sets_everything_in_one_place _____________

client = <tests.conftest.ApiClient object at 0x108d2bc50>
test_location = {'active': True, 'address': {'city': None, 'country': None, 'state': None, 'street': None, ...}, 'child_all_count': 0, 'child_count': 0, ...}
capture = <function capture.<locals>._capture at 0x10987ae80>

    def test_condemning_an_asset_sets_everything_in_one_place(
            client, test_location, capture):
        """POST /api/v1/assets/<id>/condemn — status, condition, date, reason.
    
        Until handoff 30 the app condemned with a PATCH of four fields, one of
        them a disposal DATE read off the browser's clock. This route is sent no
        date at all.
    
        The expected date is computed as **(UTC now + 1 hour).date()** and NOT as
        `date.today()`, and that is the point of the assertion rather than a
        detail of it. `date.today()` is the date on whatever machine runs this
        suite, in whatever timezone it keeps — a fact about a laptop. The server
        stamps a LAGOS day, the same day the register snapshot cuts on; stamped
        with the acting user's timezone instead (unset for every API credential,
        therefore UTC) it would date every disposal made between 00:00 and 01:00
        in Lagos to the day before. CI runs in UTC and can only see that with the
        offset written out. Taken either side of the call, so a run that
        straddles Lagos midnight asserts something true.
        """
>       asset = _new_asset(client, test_location, "pytest-events-condemn",
                           condition="fair")

            client, test_location):

test_location = {'active': True, 'address': {'city': None, 'country': None, 'state': None, 'street': None, ...}, 'child_all_count': 0, 'child_count': 0, ...}

    def test_a_date_patched_long_before_the_disposal_is_not_the_callers_date(
            client, test_location):
        """Report 31 §C2. `disposal_date` is writable on a LIVE asset.
    
        The first stamp asked "does the asset have a date?" AFTER the write,
        which cannot tell a date sent WITH the disposal from one PATCHed months
        earlier. So an asset given 2020's date while still in service and
        disposed of today was dated 2020, and the register said it left the
        estate six years before it did — a number that reconciles against
        nothing and that no finding reports, because the row looks complete.
    
        Only the date in the SAME write is the caller's. The asset ends this
        test dated, so it does not grow `test19`'s `no_disposal_date` finding.
        """
>       asset = _new_asset(client, test_location, "pytest-events-stamp-stale")

    def test_a_live_assets_disposal_date_can_still_be_cleared(

client = <tests.conftest.ApiClient object at 0x108d2bc50>

second_person = <tests.conftest.SecondPerson object at 0x108dd2f90>

    def test_a_refused_move_does_not_burn_a_transfer_reference(
            client, test_location, second_person):
        """A refusal happens before anything is written — a sequence included.
    
        `/move` draws its transfer reference from an `ir.sequence` and then
        creates the transfer. When the model refused the move of a disposed
        asset, the transfer row rolled back with the exception and the NUMBER did
        not: a sequence is not transactional. So every refused move left a gap in
        the transfer references that no transfer explained — which, in a run of
        numbered documents, is what a deleted one looks like. The route now asks
        the model's own rule before it draws anything.
    
        Proved by numbering: two real moves either side of a refused one must be
        consecutive. The suite runs serially against a tenant nobody else is
        moving assets on; if that ever stops being true this is the test that
        will say so, and it will be wrong about why.
        """
        import re

        *"a date was written"* must not be something a label can erase.
    
        TODAY's date, deliberately: the hardest case is the PATCH that looks
        most like a real verification.

____________________ test_unknown_condition_is_400_not_500 _____________________

client = <tests.conftest.ApiClient object at 0x108d2bc50>
test_location = {'active': True, 'address': {'city': None, 'country': None, 'state': None, 'street': None, ...}, 'child_all_count': 0, 'child_count': 0, ...}
capture = <function capture.<locals>._capture at 0x109035260>

    def test_unknown_condition_is_400_not_500(client, test_location, capture):
        """A value outside the `condition` enum is the caller's error, not ours.
    
        `damaged` is the value the published schema used to advertise. The ORM
        raises a bare ValueError for it, which used to reach the catch-all and
        answer 500 — a status an integration retries forever.
        """
        ts = int(time.time())
        r = client.post("/api/v1/assets", json={
            "name": f"pytest-bad-condition-{ts}",
            "category_id": 1,
            "location_id": test_location["id"],
            "condition": "damaged",
        })
        capture("assets/create_invalid_condition", r)
>       assert r.status_code == 400, f"expected 400, got {r.status_code}: {r.text[:300]}"
E       AssertionError: expected 400, got 401: {"error": {"code": "unauthorized", "message": "Invalid token", "request_id": "4f582a0bd78948d6bd9b8399f5e61590", "details": {"http_status": 401}}}
E       assert 401 == 400
E        +  where 401 = <Response [401]>.status_code

    def test_grn_filter_is_exact_not_prefix(client, test_location):
        """`?grn_reference=` must not match a longer GRN that starts with it.

    def test_non_integer_relation_id_is_400_not_500(client, test_location):
        """A non-integer many2one id is rejected by Postgres, not by the ORM.

tests/conftest.py:1034: AssertionError
______ test_the_configuration_report_answers_according_to_the_scope_held _______

client = <tests.conftest.ApiClient object at 0x108d2bc50>
token = '<jwt-redacted>'
capture = <function capture.<locals>._capture at 0x109e68680>

    def test_the_configuration_report_answers_according_to_the_scope_held(
            client, token, capture):
        """Enforced where the scope is absent; correct where it is present.
    
        **Both branches assert, and neither skips.** This began as a one-sided
        `403` test on the reasoning that "this suite's credential does not hold
        `settings:read`" — true of `jaizbankplc` and false in CI, where
        `scripts/bootstrap.py` grants its client every scope deliberately. It
        answered `200` and the test failed on a tenant that was behaving
        correctly. See `token_scopes`.
    
        The report reads `ir_config_parameter` — the table holding
        `myservice_api.jwt_secret` — so where the scope is missing the gate has
        to be real, and where it is held the REDACTION has to be real. The two
        branches are the two halves of the same control.
        """
        r = client.get(CONFIG, params={"from": "2000-01-01", "to": _iso(_today())})
        capture("reports/configuration_changes_by_scope", r)
    
        if not token_grants(token, "settings:read"):
            body = assert_envelope_err(r, 403, "forbidden")
            details = body["error"]["details"]
            assert details["required_scopes"] == ["settings:read"], (
                f"the 403 names {details.get('required_scopes')} as required; "
                "this report is gated on `settings:read` and the error must say "
                "which scope to ask for")
            assert "settings:read" in body["error"]["message"], (
                "the message does not name the missing scope, so an integrator "
                "has to read the details block to learn what to request")
            assert "granted_scopes" in details, (
                "the 403 does not say what the caller DOES hold — the caller "
                "then cannot tell a missing scope from a broken token")
            return
    
        # The credential holds it, so prove the thing the scope protects.
>       data = assert_envelope_ok(r)["data"]
               ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^

    def test_the_snapshot_says_its_filters_were_evaluated_today(client):

client = <tests.conftest.ApiClient object at 0x108d2bc50>
capture = <function capture.<locals>._capture at 0x109e6bb00>

    def test_lookup_empty_query(client, capture):
        """No query is a negative answer, not a server fault.
    
        This used to be xfailed against the `dict(<callable>)` TypeError described
        in test_lookup_with_query — wrongly: `lookup_tag` returns
        `{'found': False, ...}` before it touches any Selection field, so the
        empty-query path never reached that bug and never produced a 500. What it
        actually does, and must keep doing, is come back through
        `_delegate_failure` as an enveloped 404: a scanner pointed at nothing has
        not found a fault worth retrying.
        """
        r = client.get("/api/v1/barcode/lookup")
        capture("barcode/lookup_empty", r)
>       body = assert_envelope_err(r, 404, "not_found")

client = <tests.conftest.ApiClient object at 0x108d2bc50>
capture = <function capture.<locals>._capture at 0x109e68360>

    def test_lookup_with_query(client, capture):
        """Look up an asset by its tag — and get the domain LABEL back.
    
        This was xfailed on a real 500: `lookup_tag` did
        `dict(asset._fields['domain'].selection)`, and `nrs.asset.domain` is
        `related='category_id.domain'`, so Odoo had filled `.selection` with a
        callable and `dict()` over it raised TypeError. Every asset the lookup
        actually found in a category with an FM domain — which is most of the
        register — answered 500. It is fixed by resolving the label through
        `fields_get`, which does not care how the field was declared.
    
        The label, not the key, is the assertion that matters: returning `"it"`
        where `"IT & Communications"` belongs would be a green 200 and a barcode
        station showing raw enum keys to a field team.
        """
>       asset = _asset_with_an_fm_domain(client)
                ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^

    def test_the_suite_is_running_where_it_is_allowed_to(client):

tests/conftest.py:1034: AssertionError
_____________ test_a_category_carries_its_own_verification_window ______________

client = <tests.conftest.ApiClient object at 0x108d2bc50>, admin_scope = True

    def test_a_category_carries_its_own_verification_window(client, admin_scope):
        """`verification_interval_days` is writable, readable and survives a PATCH.
    
        `is_verified` used to be measured against a hardcoded 365 days that nothing
        ever re-evaluated, so it could only move false -> true: an asset verified
        once was reported verified for ever. It now expires on a per-category
        window, and this is the route a tenant sets that window from — the same
        screen, and the same shape, as `pm_interval_days` beside it.
    
        0 is not "verified only if verified today". It is the sentinel for "use the
        tenant default", which is why the API reports it as null rather than 0.
        """
        ts = int(time.time())
        r = client.post("/api/v1/asset-categories", json={
            "name": f"pytest-verify-{ts}",
            "code": f"PV{ts % 10000}",
            "domain": "other",
            "verification_interval_days": 90,
        })
>       created = assert_envelope_ok(r, expected_status=201)["data"]
                  ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^

tests/test_depreciation_settings.py:158:

    def assert_envelope_ok(response, expected_status=200):

    def test_the_label_cannot_be_set_by_the_caller(client, test_location):
        """Sending it is REFUSED, not honoured — and not dropped either.
    
        `is_test_fixture` is in no `_WRITABLE_FIELDS`, so a body that names it is
        a 400 naming it back: the published behaviour, since handoff 31, for a
        field the API will not accept. Asserted rather than assumed: a label a
        caller can send is a label that says whatever the caller wants, and
        `tests/test_source_guards.py` guards the allow-list from the other side.
    
        Until handoff 31 this was a 201 — the location was CREATED, labelled
        correctly, with the claim listed in `_warnings.rejected_fields`. The
        label was safe either way. What changed is that a caller who sent
        `is_test_fixture: false` believing it had declared real data is now told
        that it cannot, instead of being handed a record that says the opposite.
    
        The control is the second half: the same name WITHOUT the claim is
        created, found, and labelled from the credential.
        """

        name = "pytest-loc-label-probe-%d" % int(time.time() * 1000)

    def test_overlong_key_is_rejected(client, test_location, capture):
        """A key over 128 chars must 400, not silently degrade to a plain create.

tests/conftest.py:1034: AssertionError
_________________ test_concurrent_same_key_creates_exactly_one _________________

client = <tests.conftest.ApiClient object at 0x108d2bc50>
test_location = {'active': True, 'address': {'city': None, 'country': None, 'state': None, 'street': None, ...}, 'child_all_count': 0, 'child_count': 0, ...}
capture = <function capture.<locals>._capture at 0x10e0b72e0>

    def test_concurrent_same_key_creates_exactly_one(client, test_location, capture):
        """Four simultaneous creates under one key must yield one asset.
    
        This is the case the key exists for: a client retrying a request whose
        response it never saw. The requests genuinely race — the losers do create
        an asset before failing to claim the key, and the handler has to discard
        that work rather than keep it and answer with the winner's body.
    
        Whether the race is actually hit depends on worker scheduling, so the
        assertions are on the invariant (one asset, one id, always an envelope)
        rather than on the race occurring.
        """
        import concurrent.futures
    
        key = f"pytest-idem-race-{int(time.time() * 1000)}"
        name = f"idem-race-{key}"
        payload = {"name": name, "category_id": 1, "location_id": test_location["id"]}
    
        def fire(_):
            return client.post("/api/v1/assets", json=payload,
                               headers={"X-Idempotency-Key": key})
    
        with concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=4) as pool:
            responses = list(pool.map(fire, range(4)))
        capture("idempotency/concurrent_same_key", responses[0])
    
        created_ids = set()
        for r in responses:
            # A raw 500 with an HTML or empty body is the failure mode this
            # guards: every outcome has to be the PRD envelope.
            body = r.json()
            assert "data" in body or "error" in body, f"non-enveloped response: {r.text[:300]}"
>           assert r.status_code in (200, 201, 409), (
                f"unexpected status {r.status_code}: {r.text[:300]}"
            )
E           AssertionError: unexpected status 401: {"error": {"code": "unauthorized", "message": "Invalid token", "request_id": "648c4f9f273546d682afb475285ae933", "details": {"http_status": 401}}}
E           assert 401 in (200, 201, 409)
E            +  where 401 = <Response [401]>.status_code

client = <tests.conftest.ApiClient object at 0x108d2bc50>

    def test_key_is_accepted_and_ignored_where_it_is_not_honoured(client):
        """A well-formed key on a read route is accepted AND genuinely ignored.
    
        Two assertions, because "accepted" alone proves nothing — a GET never
        reached the key handling before this change either, so asserting a 200
        would pass just as well against reverted code. What is worth pinning is
        the second half of the contract: the response is recomputed, not replayed
        from the cache, so a caller spraying the header cannot accidentally opt
        into caching on an endpoint the docs list as ignoring it.
        """
        key = f"pytest-spray-{int(time.time())}-{uuid.uuid4().hex[:8]}"
>       first = assert_envelope_ok(
            client.get("/api/v1/assets", params={"limit": 1},
                       headers={"X-Idempotency-Key": key}))

tests/conftest.py:1049: AssertionError
____________________________ test_sap_employee_sync ____________________________

client = <tests.conftest.ApiClient object at 0x108d2bc50>
capture = <function capture.<locals>._capture at 0x109d6fd80>

    def test_sap_employee_sync(client, capture):
        """POST /api/v1/integrations/sap/employee-sync — minimal SAP-shaped payload.
    
        The handler delegates to nrs.sap.sync.log.process_sap_payload(...) which
        has its own validation. We send a deliberately minimal payload; either
        a success envelope OR a model-side validation error is acceptable, as
        long as the API envelope shape is right.
    
        **`status` used to be the last line of this payload** and was never once
        read: `nrs.sap.sync.log` takes `employmentStatus` or `sap_status`, and
        the route dropped anything else in silence and answered 200 (handoff 32
        §B). The published `SapEmployee` schema, the endpoint reference's
        canonical example and the demo card all sent it too. An unaccepted key
        is now a 400 naming it — see `tests/test_the_twelfth_door.py` — so this
        payload is corrected to the key the model actually reads.
        """
        r = client.post("/api/v1/integrations/sap/employee-sync", json={
            "ir_number": "PYTEST-IRTEST-001",
            "userId": "pytest.user@example.com",
            "firstName": "Py",
            "lastName": "Test",
            "department": "Pytest",
            "jobTitle": "Smoke Tester",
            "employmentStatus": "active",
        })
        capture("integrations/sap_employee_sync", r)
        # PRD envelope, success or error
>       assert r.status_code in (200, 201, 400, 500), f"unexpected status {r.status_code}"
E       AssertionError: unexpected status 401
E       assert 401 in (200, 201, 400, 500)
E        +  where 401 = <Response [401]>.status_code

tests/conftest.py:1034: AssertionError
___________________________ test_list_filtered_by_q ____________________________

client = <tests.conftest.ApiClient object at 0x108d2bc50>
capture = <function capture.<locals>._capture at 0x109d6ec00>

    def test_list_filtered_by_q(client, capture):
        """GET /api/v1/locations?q=Lagos — narrowing filter.
    
        `?q=` searches FOUR fields — `name`, `description`, `zone_code` and the
        stored city (`v1_locations._list_domain`) — and this test asserted the
        match was always in the name. It passed for the life of the suite because
        no location on the test tenant happened to be in a city called Lagos.
        Handoff 32's sweep created one — *Sweep probe location*, city Lagos — and
        the test failed on a row the filter was right to return.
    
        A test that only passes because of the data it happens to meet is a test
        of the tenant, not of the route. It now asserts what the route actually
        promises: the term appears in one of the fields the route searches.
        """
        r = client.get("/api/v1/locations", params={"q": "Lagos", "limit": 5})
        capture("locations/list_filtered", r)
>       body = assert_envelope_ok(r)
               ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^

    @pytest.mark.parametrize("path", LIST_ENDPOINTS)
    def test_total_estimate_is_never_the_page_size(client, path, capture):
        """The count is a fact about the register, not about the response.
    
        The old bug is invisible on a small resource — `total_estimate` and the
        row count agree when nothing was withheld. It is only detectable where
        the cap actually bit, so this asserts the RELATIONSHIP: if a truncation
        was declared, the total must exceed what was returned.
        """

        capture(f"pagination/{path.strip('/').replace('/', '_')}_total", r)

    @pytest.mark.parametrize("path", LIST_ENDPOINTS)
    def test_total_estimate_is_never_the_page_size(client, path, capture):
        """The count is a fact about the register, not about the response.
    
        The old bug is invisible on a small resource — `total_estimate` and the
        row count agree when nothing was withheld. It is only detectable where
        the cap actually bit, so this asserts the RELATIONSHIP: if a truncation
        was declared, the total must exceed what was returned.

______________ test_the_warning_describes_the_QUERY_not_the_page _______________

client = <tests.conftest.ApiClient object at 0x108d2bc50>
capture = <function capture.<locals>._capture at 0x109d19440>

    def test_the_warning_describes_the_QUERY_not_the_page(client, capture):
        """A small page of an over-cap query still warns — on page one.
    
        This is a deliberate choice and it is worth stating plainly. The cap
        bites on the query, not on the page: a caller walking a 2,383-asset
        register five rows at a time will hit the wall at record 100. Declaring
        the truncation only on the page where the wall is reached would tell
        them at the end of a twenty-page walk what they needed to know before
        they started.
        """
        r = client.get("/api/v1/assets?limit=5")
        capture("pagination/assets_small_page", r)
>       body = assert_envelope_ok(r)
               ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^

tests/conftest.py:1034: AssertionError
_ test_a_field_the_route_does_not_accept_is_refused_and_nothing_is_written[POST /assets] _

request = <FixtureRequest for <Function test_a_field_the_route_does_not_accept_is_refused_and_nothing_is_written[POST /assets]>>
label = 'POST /assets', build = <function _post_asset at 0x1095d4900>

    @pytest.mark.parametrize("label,build", _ROUTES,
                             ids=[label for label, _ in _ROUTES])
    def test_a_field_the_route_does_not_accept_is_refused_and_nothing_is_written(
            request, label, build):
        """One answer on all ten, and the answer is for the whole request.
    
        For a PATCH the refused key is sent ALONE, which is also the case that
        used to be the quietest defect of the lot: a PATCH whose every field was
        refused passed the empty-body check, wrote nothing, and answered 200 —
        typed, saved, unchanged, no message.
    
        For a create the refused key rides beside the fields the route requires,
        so the body is one the route would otherwise accept; "nothing written"
        is then that no record of that name exists.
    
        `approve-disposal` reads no body at all, so ANY key is refused and
        `writable` is the empty list: the repair is to send nothing.
        """
        row = build(request)
>       before = row["read"]()
                 ^^^^^^^^^^^^^

    @pytest.mark.parametrize("label,build", _ROUTES,
                             ids=[label for label, _ in _ROUTES])
    def test_a_field_the_route_does_not_accept_is_refused_and_nothing_is_written(
            request, label, build):
        """One answer on all ten, and the answer is for the whole request.
    
        For a PATCH the refused key is sent ALONE, which is also the case that
        used to be the quietest defect of the lot: a PATCH whose every field was
        refused passed the empty-body check, wrote nothing, and answered 200 —
        typed, saved, unchanged, no message.
    
        For a create the refused key rides beside the fields the route requires,
        so the body is one the route would otherwise accept; "nothing written"
        is then that no record of that name exists.
    
        `approve-disposal` reads no body at all, so ANY key is refused and
        `writable` is the empty list: the repair is to send nothing.
        """

label = 'PATCH /settings/branding'
build = <function _patch_branding at 0x1095d4ea0>

    @pytest.mark.parametrize("label,build", _ROUTES,
                             ids=[label for label, _ in _ROUTES])
    def test_a_field_the_route_does_not_accept_is_refused_and_nothing_is_written(
            request, label, build):
        """One answer on all ten, and the answer is for the whole request.
    
        For a PATCH the refused key is sent ALONE, which is also the case that
        used to be the quietest defect of the lot: a PATCH whose every field was
        refused passed the empty-body check, wrote nothing, and answered 200 —
        typed, saved, unchanged, no message.
    
        For a create the refused key rides beside the fields the route requires,
        so the body is one the route would otherwise accept; "nothing written"
        is then that no record of that name exists.
    
        `approve-disposal` reads no body at all, so ANY key is refused and
        `writable` is the empty list: the repair is to send nothing.
        """
>       row = build(request)

    @pytest.mark.parametrize("label,build", _CREATE_ROUTES,

tests/conftest.py:1034: AssertionError
_ test_a_body_that_mixes_a_good_field_and_a_refused_one_writes_neither[PATCH /assets/<id>] _

request = <FixtureRequest for <Function test_a_body_that_mixes_a_good_field_and_a_refused_one_writes_neither[PATCH /assets/<id>]>>
label = 'PATCH /assets/<id>', build = <function _patch_asset at 0x1095d4a40>

    @pytest.mark.parametrize("label,build", _PATCH_ROUTES,
                             ids=[label for label, _ in _PATCH_ROUTES])
    def test_a_body_that_mixes_a_good_field_and_a_refused_one_writes_neither(
            request, label, build):
        """Refused WHOLE — the half of the change that is easiest to get wrong.
    
        Handoff 30 had it the other way on purpose: *refused, not rejected
        whole*, so the writable field beside a refused one still landed. It was
        reversed because of what the caller does next. Told 400, it fixes the
        body and sends ALL of it again; told 200, it never learns that half of
        what it sent was dropped. And `err()` is a response, not a rollback
        (CLAUDE.md) — so this is only true if the route refuses BEFORE it
        writes, which is what is proved here by reading.
    
        The good half is proved good: sent ALONE, afterwards, it lands. Without
        that, a `good` value the route would have refused anyway makes the first
        half pass for the wrong reason. The two settings rows put back what that
        second call changed.
        """
>       row = build(request)
              ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^

            request, label, build):
        """Refused WHOLE — the half of the change that is easiest to get wrong.
    
        Handoff 30 had it the other way on purpose: *refused, not rejected
        whole*, so the writable field beside a refused one still landed. It was
        reversed because of what the caller does next. Told 400, it fixes the
        body and sends ALL of it again; told 200, it never learns that half of
        what it sent was dropped. And `err()` is a response, not a rollback
        (CLAUDE.md) — so this is only true if the route refuses BEFORE it
        writes, which is what is proved here by reading.
    
        The good half is proved good: sent ALONE, afterwards, it lands. Without
        that, a `good` value the route would have refused anyway makes the first
        half pass for the wrong reason. The two settings rows put back what that
        second call changed.
        """
>       row = build(request)
              ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^

tests/conftest.py:1034: AssertionError
___________ test_a_refused_create_does_not_burn_its_idempotency_key ____________

client = <tests.conftest.ApiClient object at 0x108d2bc50>
test_location = {'active': True, 'address': {'city': None, 'country': None, 'state': None, 'street': None, ...}, 'child_all_count': 0, 'child_count': 0, ...}

    def test_a_refused_create_does_not_burn_its_idempotency_key(
            client, test_location):
        """The sequence Vendra needs: fix the mapper, deliver again under the
        SAME key, and it lands.
    
        Only a SUCCESS is stored under an `X-Idempotency-Key`. So a refused
        request is evaluated afresh each time — the second 400 is a new answer
        with a new `request_id`, not a replay — and the corrected body under the
        same key is processed, creates the asset, and from then on IS replayed.
    
        This is the defect §B was ruled to close, from the other side. When the
        lenient answer was a 201, the key pinned it: every retry replayed the
        same incomplete asset, and the only repair was by hand.
        """
        key = _uniq("pytest-refused-idem")
        name = _uniq("pytest-refused-idem-asset")
        good = {"name": name, "category_id": 1,
                "location_id": test_location["id"]}
        headers = {"X-Idempotency-Key": key}
    
        first = client.post("/api/v1/assets", json={**good, _KEY: 1},
                            headers=headers)
>       assert_fields_refused(first, _KEY)

    def test_has_sap_code_rejects_a_value_it_cannot_interpret(client, capture):
        """`?has_sap_code=1` must 400, not return the unfiltered register.

tests/conftest.py:1034: AssertionError
__________________ test_sap_fields_are_not_writable_on_create __________________

client = <tests.conftest.ApiClient object at 0x108d2bc50>
capture = <function capture.<locals>._capture at 0x109c64400>

    def test_sap_fields_are_not_writable_on_create(client, capture):
        """A client that can set `sap_asset_number` can fake a reconciliation.
    
        The dual-code control is an audit control: `reconciled` is supposed to
        mean finance booked the asset. A caller able to POST the number would
        reach that state without SAP having booked anything, and a caller able to
        POST the state would reach it without even the pretence of a code. Both
        are REFUSED — rule 3, and since handoff 31 with a 400 rather than a 201
        and a warning: until then the asset was created WITHOUT the number, which
        is what Vendra dead-lettered after the fact, under an idempotency key
        that replayed the same incomplete 201 on every retry.
    
        So this asserts two records: the refused body created NONE, and the same
        asset sent without the four fields is created with an empty SAP block.
        """
        name = f"pytest-sapwrite-{_uniq()}"
        r = client.post("/api/v1/assets", json={
            "name": name,
            "category_id": 1,
            "sap_asset_number": "100000001234",
            "sap_reconciliation_state": "reconciled",
            "sap_code_source": "integration",
            "sap_synced_at": "2026-09-06T10:00:00",
        })
        capture("sap/create_rejects_sap_fields", r)
>       assert_fields_refused(r, "sap_asset_number", "sap_reconciliation_state",
                              "sap_code_source", "sap_synced_at")

client = <tests.conftest.ApiClient object at 0x108d2bc50>

    def test_asset_tag_is_not_writable_on_create(client):
        """A client that can set `tag` can walk the record off the physical label.
    
        It would then fail the affix check it just defeated, with a 400 that looks
        like the scanner's fault. Breaking change, announced: Vendra is the only
        live consumer and does not write this field.
    
        REFUSED since handoff 31, where it was dropped beside a 201: the asset
        was created under a tag Cadra minted, and the caller that believed it
        had chosen one found out at the affix check. Nothing is created now, and
        the same asset without the tag is.
        """
        name = f"pytest-tagwrite-{_uniq()}"
        r = client.post("/api/v1/assets", json={
            "name": name, "category_id": 1,
            "asset_tag": "OPERATOR-CHOSEN-TAG"})
>       assert_fields_refused(r, "asset_tag")

____________ test_a_tag_awaiting_replacement_still_reads_as_tagged _____________

client = <tests.conftest.ApiClient object at 0x108d2bc50>

    def test_a_tag_awaiting_replacement_still_reads_as_tagged(client):
        """Requesting a replacement does not peel the label off the asset.
    
        `active_tag_id` drops a tag the moment its state becomes `pending`. If

        named for acceptance criterion 1 — while asserting `printed_at is None`
        twice, both assertions negative. It never printed anything, and neither
        did anything else in this repository: `/report/pdf/` is `auth='user'` and
        this suite holds a bearer token, not an Odoo session, so it cannot render
        a report at all. A test that cannot reach the funnel cannot cover it.

        an identity is not the same event as issuing a print instruction — is
        real and is the reason `label_state` exists separately from `state`.

        `myservice_asset_register/tests/test_print_funnel.py`, which drives every
        real caller through `_render_qweb_pdf` and asserts the stamp, including
        once against wkhtmltopdf for a genuine PDF.

tests/conftest.py:1034: AssertionError
______ test_the_literal_string_null_is_rejected_not_treated_as_no_filter _______

client = <tests.conftest.ApiClient object at 0x108d2bc50>
capture = <function capture.<locals>._capture at 0x109b45760>

    def test_the_literal_string_null_is_rejected_not_treated_as_no_filter(client, capture):
        """`?tag_label_state=null` must 400, not hand back the whole register.
    
        `"null"` is the house sentinel for "not supplied" on the id and boolean
        filters, and reusing it here looked consistent — but the two failures are
        not symmetric. A caller who sends `null` for `is_tagged` wanted no
        tagging filter and gets none. A caller who sends it here is a JS poller
        that stringified an unset value, and answering "no filter" hands them
        every asset in the register while they believe they are looking at one
        label state.
    
        An empty list reads as "no matches"; a full list reads as an answer. So
        this is the more dangerous direction of the same silent-degradation bug
        the enum check exists to close, and it validates like any other
        unrecognised value.
        """
        r = client.get("/api/v1/assets", params={"tag_label_state": "null"})
        capture("assets/tag_label_state_null", r)
>       body = assert_envelope_err(r, expected_status=400,
                                   expected_code="invalid_request")
